* [e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Anarchism_sidebar "Special:EditPage/Template:Anarchism sidebar")                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

**Anarchism** is a [political philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_philosophy "Political philosophy") and [movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_movement "Political movement") that is against all forms of authority and seeks to abolish the institutions it claims maintain unnecessary coercion and [hierarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_hierarchy "Social hierarchy"), typically including the [state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_\(polity\) "State (polity)") and [capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitalism "Capitalism"). Anarchism advocates for the replacement of the state with [stateless societies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stateless_society "Stateless society") and voluntary [free associations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_association_\(communism_and_anarchism\) "Free association (communism and anarchism)"). A historically left-wing movement, anarchism is usually described as the [libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian "Libertarian") wing of the [socialist movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_movement "Socialist movement") ([libertarian socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian_socialism "Libertarian socialism")).

Although traces of anarchist ideas are found all throughout history, modern anarchism emerged from the [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment "Age of Enlightenment"). During the latter half of the 19th and the first decades of the 20th century, the anarchist movement flourished in most parts of the world and had a significant role in [workers' struggles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_movement "Labour movement") for [emancipation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emancipation "Emancipation"). [Various anarchist schools of thought](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#Schools_of_thought) formed during this period. Anarchists have taken part in [several revolutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_revolutions_and_rebellions "List of revolutions and rebellions"), most notably in the [Paris Commune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Commune "Paris Commune"), the [Russian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Civil_War "Russian Civil War") and the [Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War "Spanish Civil War"), whose end marked the end of the [classical era of anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_era_of_anarchism "Classical era of anarchism"). In the last decades of the 20th and into the 21st century, the anarchist movement has been resurgent once more, growing in popularity and influence within [anti-capitalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-capitalist "Anti-capitalist"), [anti-war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-war "Anti-war") and [anti-globalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-globalisation "Anti-globalisation") movements.

Anarchists employ [diverse approaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diversity_of_tactics "Diversity of tactics"), which may be generally divided into [revolutionary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary "Revolutionary") and [evolutionary strategies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolutionary_strategies "Evolutionary strategies"); there is significant overlap between the two. Evolutionary methods try to simulate what an anarchist society might be like, but revolutionary tactics, which have historically taken a [violent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violent_extremism "Violent extremism") turn, aim to overthrow authority and the state. Many facets of [human civilization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilization "Civilization") have been influenced by anarchist theory, critique, and [praxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Praxis_\(process\) "Praxis (process)").

## Etymology, terminology, and definition

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=1 "Edit section: Etymology, terminology, and definition")]

Main article: [Definition of anarchism and libertarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Definition_of_anarchism_and_libertarianism "Definition of anarchism and libertarianism")

See also: [Glossary of anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_anarchism "Glossary of anarchism")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/86/WilhelmWeitling.jpg/220px-WilhelmWeitling.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:WilhelmWeitling.jpg)

[Wilhelm Weitling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Weitling "Wilhelm Weitling") is an example of a writer who added to anarchist theory without using the exact term.[\[1\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarlson197222%E2%80%9323-1)

The [etymological](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etymology "Etymology") origin of *anarchism* is from the [Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek "Ancient Greek") *anarkhia* (ἀναρχία), meaning "without a ruler", composed of the [prefix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefix "Prefix") *an-* ("without") and the word *arkhos* ("leader" or "ruler"). The [suffix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suffix "Suffix") *[-ism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/-ism "-ism")* denotes the ideological current that favours anarchy.[\[2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBates2017128Long2013217-2) *Anarchism* appears in English from 1642 as *anarchisme* and *anarchy* from 1539; early English usages emphasised a sense of disorder.[\[3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMerriam-Webster2019%22Anarchism%22''Oxford_English_Dictionary''2005%22Anarchism%22Sylvan2007260-3) Various factions within the [French Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution "French Revolution") labelled their opponents as *anarchists*, although few such accused shared many views with later anarchists. Many revolutionaries of the 19th century such as [William Godwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Godwin "William Godwin") (1756–1836) and [Wilhelm Weitling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Weitling "Wilhelm Weitling") (1808–1871) would contribute to the anarchist doctrines of the next generation but did not use *anarchist* or *anarchism* in describing themselves or their beliefs.[\[4\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJoll196427%E2%80%9337-4)

The first [political philosopher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_philosophers "List of political philosophers") to call himself an *anarchist* (French: *anarchiste*) was [Pierre-Joseph Proudhon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Joseph_Proudhon "Pierre-Joseph Proudhon") (1809–1865),[\[5\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKahn2000-5) marking the formal birth of anarchism in the mid-19th century. Since the 1890s and beginning in France,[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTENettlau1996162-6) *[libertarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarianism "Libertarianism")* has often been used as a synonym for anarchism;[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGu%C3%A9rin1970%22The_Basic_Ideas_of_Anarchism%22-7) its use as a synonym is still common outside the United States.[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWard200462Goodway20064Skirda2002183Fern%C3%A1ndez20099-8) Some usages of *libertarianism* refer to [individualistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individualistic "Individualistic") [free-market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-market "Free-market") philosophy only, and [free-market anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-market_anarchism "Free-market anarchism") in particular is termed *libertarian anarchism*.[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris200261-9)

While the term *libertarian* has been largely synonymous with anarchism,[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992641Cohn20096-10) its meaning has more recently been diluted by wider adoption from ideologically disparate groups,[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992641-11) including both the [New Left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Left "New Left") and [libertarian Marxists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian_Marxists "Libertarian Marxists"), who do not associate themselves with [authoritarian socialists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarian_socialists "Authoritarian socialists") or a [vanguard party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanguard_party "Vanguard party"), and extreme [cultural liberals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_liberals "Cultural liberals"), who are primarily concerned with [civil liberties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_liberties "Civil liberties").[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992641-11) Additionally, some anarchists use *[libertarian socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian_socialist "Libertarian socialist")*[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992641Cohn20096LevyAdams2018104-12) to avoid anarchism's negative connotations and emphasise its connections with [socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism "Socialism").[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992641-11) *Anarchism* is broadly used to describe the [anti-authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-authoritarian "Anti-authoritarian") wing of the [socialist movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_movement "Socialist movement").[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevyAdams2018104-13)[\[nb 1\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-17) Anarchism is contrasted to socialist forms which are [state-oriented](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_socialism "State socialism") or from above.[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOsgood18891-18) Scholars of anarchism generally highlight anarchism's socialist credentials[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTENewman200515-19) and criticise attempts at creating dichotomies between the two.[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris201564-20) Some scholars describe anarchism as having many influences from liberalism,[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992641-11) and being both liberal and socialist but more so.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalter200244-21) Many scholars reject [anarcho-capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-capitalism "Anarcho-capitalism") as a misunderstanding of anarchist principles.[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992564%E2%80%93565Jennings1993143GayGay199915Morris200813Johnson2008169Franks2013393%E2%80%93394-22)[\[nb 2\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-24)

While [opposition to the state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-statism "Anti-statism") is central to anarchist thought, defining *anarchism* is not an easy task for scholars, as there is a lot of discussion among scholars and anarchists on the matter, and various currents perceive anarchism slightly differently.[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELong2013217-25)[\[nb 3\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-28) Major definitional elements include the will for a non-coercive society, the rejection of the state apparatus, the belief that [human nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_nature "Human nature") allows humans to exist in or progress toward such a non-coercive society, and a suggestion on how to act to pursue the ideal of anarchy.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLaughlin200725%E2%80%9326-29)

## History

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=2 "Edit section: History")]

Main article: [History of anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_anarchism "History of anarchism")

### Pre-modern era

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=3 "Edit section: Pre-modern era")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Napoli%2C_1969%29_-_BEIC_6353768.jpg/150px-Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_%28Napoli%2C_1969%29_-_BEIC_6353768.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Paolo_Monti_-_Servizio_fotografico_\(Napoli,_1969\)_-_BEIC_6353768.jpg)

[Zeno of Citium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zeno_of_Citium "Zeno of Citium") (c. 334 – c. 262 BC), whose *[Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_\(Zeno\) "Republic (Zeno)")* inspired [Peter Kropotkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Kropotkin "Peter Kropotkin")[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall199370-30)

The most notable precursors to anarchism in the ancient world were in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_China#Ancient_China "History of China") and [Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greece "Ancient Greece"). In China, [philosophical anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophical_anarchism "Philosophical anarchism") (the discussion on the legitimacy of the state) was delineated by [Taoist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism "Taoism") philosophers [Zhuang Zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhuang_Zhou "Zhuang Zhou") and [Laozi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laozi "Laozi").[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTECoutinho2016Marshall199354-31) Alongside [Stoicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoicism "Stoicism"), Taoism has been said to have had "significant anticipations" of anarchism.[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTESylvan2007257-32)

Anarchic attitudes were also articulated by [tragedians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tragedy "Tragedy") and [philosophers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophy "Philosophy") in Greece. [Aeschylus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeschylus "Aeschylus") and [Sophocles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophocles "Sophocles") used the myth of [Antigone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antigone "Antigone") to illustrate the conflict between laws imposed by the state and personal [autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomy "Autonomy"). [Socrates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socrates "Socrates") questioned [Athenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athens "Athens") authorities constantly and insisted on the right of individual [freedom of conscience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_conscience "Freedom of conscience"). [Cynics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cynicism_\(philosophy\) "Cynicism (philosophy)") dismissed human law (*[nomos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nomos_\(sociology\) "Nomos (sociology)")*) and associated authorities while trying to live according to nature (*[physis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physis "Physis")*). [Stoics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stoics "Stoics") were supportive of a society based on unofficial and friendly relations among its citizens without the presence of a state.[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall19934,_66%E2%80%9373-33)

In [medieval Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages "Middle Ages"), there was no anarchistic activity except some ascetic religious movements. These, and other Muslim movements, later gave birth to [religious anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_and_religion "Anarchism and religion"). In the [Sasanian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasanian_Empire "Sasanian Empire"), [Mazdak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazdak "Mazdak") called for an [egalitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egalitarian "Egalitarian") society and the [abolition of monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abolition_of_monarchy "Abolition of monarchy"), only to be soon executed by Emperor [Kavad I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kavad_I "Kavad I").[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall199386-34) In [Basra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basra "Basra"), religious sects preached against the state.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTECrone20003,_21%E2%80%9325-35) In Europe, various religious sects developed anti-state and libertarian tendencies.[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTENettlau19968-36)

Renewed interest in antiquity during the [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance "Renaissance") and in private judgment during the [Reformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reformation "Reformation") restored elements of anti-authoritarian [secularism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism "Secularism") in Europe, particularly in France.[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993108-37) [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment "Age of Enlightenment") challenges to intellectual authority (secular and religious) and the [revolutions of the 1790s and 1848](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_revolutions_and_rebellions "List of revolutions and rebellions") all spurred the ideological development of what became the era of classical anarchism.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevyAdams2018307-38)

### Modern era

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=4 "Edit section: Modern era")]

During the [French Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution "French Revolution"), partisan groups such as the [Enragés](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrag%C3%A9s "Enragés") and the *[sans-culottes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sans-culottes "Sans-culottes")* saw a turning point in the fermentation of anti-state and federalist sentiments.[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall19934-39) The first anarchist currents developed throughout the 18th century as [William Godwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Godwin "William Godwin") espoused [philosophical anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philosophical_anarchism "Philosophical anarchism") [in England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_the_United_Kingdom "Anarchism in the United Kingdom"), morally delegitimising the state, [Max Stirner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Stirner "Max Stirner")'s thinking paved the way to [individualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individualist_anarchism "Individualist anarchism") and [Pierre-Joseph Proudhon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Joseph_Proudhon "Pierre-Joseph Proudhon")'s theory of [mutualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutualism_\(economic_theory\) "Mutualism (economic theory)") found fertile soil [in France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_France "Anarchism in France").[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall19934%E2%80%935-40) By the late 1870s, various anarchist schools of thought had become well-defined and a wave of then-unprecedented [globalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalisation "Globalisation") occurred from 1880 to 1914.[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy201110%E2%80%9315-41) This era of [classical anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_anarchism "Classical anarchism") lasted until the end of the [Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War "Spanish Civil War") and is considered the golden age of anarchism.[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall19934%E2%80%935-40)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Bakunin.png/170px-Bakunin.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bakunin.png)

Mikhail Bakunin opposed the Marxist aim of [dictatorship of the proletariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictatorship_of_the_proletariat "Dictatorship of the proletariat") and allied himself with the federalists in the First International before his expulsion by the Marxists.

Drawing from mutualism, [Mikhail Bakunin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Bakunin "Mikhail Bakunin") founded [collectivist anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivist_anarchism "Collectivist anarchism") and entered the [International Workingmen's Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Workingmen%27s_Association "International Workingmen's Association"), a class [worker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Working_class "Working class") [union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_union "Trade union") later known as the [First International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Workingmen%27s_Association "International Workingmen's Association") that formed in 1864 to unite diverse revolutionary currents. The International became a significant political force, with [Karl Marx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Marx "Karl Marx") being a leading figure and a member of its General Council. Bakunin's faction (the [Jura Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jura_Federation "Jura Federation")) and Proudhon's followers (the mutualists) opposed [state socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_socialism "State socialism"), advocating political [abstentionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abstentionism "Abstentionism") and small property holdings.[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDodson2002312Thomas1985187ChaliandBlin2007116-42) After bitter disputes, the Bakuninists were expelled from the International by the [Marxists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxists "Marxists") at the [1872 Hague Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1872_Hague_Congress "1872 Hague Congress").[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham2019334%E2%80%93336Marshall199324-43) Anarchists were treated similarly in the [Second International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_International "Second International"), being ultimately expelled in 1896.[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy201112-44) Bakunin predicted that if revolutionaries gained power by Marx's terms, they would end up the [new tyrants of workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictatorship_of_the_proletariat "Dictatorship of the proletariat"). In response to their expulsion from the First International, anarchists formed the [St. Imier International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Imier_International "St. Imier International"). Under the influence of [Peter Kropotkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Kropotkin "Peter Kropotkin"), a Russian philosopher and scientist, [anarcho-communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-communism "Anarcho-communism") overlapped with [collectivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivist_anarchism "Collectivist anarchism").[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall19935-45) Anarcho-communists, who drew inspiration from the 1871 [Paris Commune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Commune "Paris Commune"), advocated for free federation and for the distribution of goods [according to one's needs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_each_according_to_his_ability,_to_each_according_to_his_needs "From each according to his ability, to each according to his needs").[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham2005xii-46)

By the turn of the 20th century, anarchism had spread all over the world.[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoya2015327-47) It was a notable feature of the international [syndicalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syndicalism "Syndicalism") movement.[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy201116-48) In [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_China "Anarchism in China"), small groups of students imported the [humanistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanism "Humanism") pro-science version of anarcho-communism.[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993519%E2%80%93521-49) [Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Japan "Anarchism in Japan") was a hotspot for [rebellious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebellion "Rebellion") youth from East Asian countries, who moved to the Japanese capital to study.[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDirlik1991133Ramnath2019681%E2%80%93682-50) In Latin America, [Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Argentina "Anarchism in Argentina") was a stronghold for [anarcho-syndicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-syndicalism "Anarcho-syndicalism"), where it became the most prominent left-wing ideology.[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy201123Laursen2019157Marshall1993504%E2%80%93508-51) During this time, a minority of anarchists adopted tactics of revolutionary [political violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_violence "Political violence"), known as [propaganda of the deed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propaganda_of_the_deed "Propaganda of the deed").[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993633%E2%80%93636-52) The dismemberment of the French socialist movement into many groups and the execution and exile of many [Communards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communards "Communards") to [penal colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penal_colony "Penal colony") following the suppression of the Paris Commune favoured individualist political expression and acts.[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnderson2004-53) Even though many anarchists distanced themselves from these terrorist acts, infamy came upon the movement and attempts were made to prevent [anarchists immigrating to the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_the_United_States "Anarchism in the United States"), including the [Immigration Act of 1903](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_Act_of_1903 "Immigration Act of 1903"), also called the Anarchist Exclusion Act.[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993633%E2%80%93636LutzUlmschneider201946-54) [Illegalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegalism "Illegalism") was another strategy which some anarchists adopted during this period.[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBantman2019374-55)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Makhno_group.jpg/220px-Makhno_group.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Makhno_group.jpg)

Nestor Makhno seen with members of the anarchist [Revolutionary Insurgent Army of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_Insurgent_Army_of_Ukraine "Revolutionary Insurgent Army of Ukraine")

Despite concerns, [anarchists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Russia "Anarchism in Russia") enthusiastically participated in the [Russian Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Revolution "Russian Revolution") in opposition to the [White movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_movement "White movement"), especially in the [Makhnovshchina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makhnovshchina "Makhnovshchina"); however, they met harsh suppression after the [Bolshevik government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshevik_government "Bolshevik government") had stabilised, including during the [Kronstadt rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kronstadt_rebellion "Kronstadt rebellion").[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvrich2006204-56) Several anarchists from Petrograd and Moscow fled to Ukraine, before the Bolsheviks crushed the [anarchist movement there](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Ukraine "Anarchism in Ukraine") too.[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvrich2006204-56) With the anarchists being repressed in Russia, two new antithetical currents emerged, namely [platformism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platformism "Platformism") and [synthesis anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synthesis_anarchism "Synthesis anarchism"). The former sought to create a coherent group that would push for revolution while the latter were against anything that would resemble a political party. Seeing the victories of the [Bolsheviks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsheviks "Bolsheviks") in the [October Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_Revolution "October Revolution") and the resulting [Russian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Civil_War "Russian Civil War"), many workers and activists turned to [communist parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_parties "Communist parties"), which grew at the expense of anarchism and other socialist movements. In France and the United States, members of major syndicalist movements such as the [General Confederation of Labour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Confederation_of_Labour_\(France\) "General Confederation of Labour (France)") and the [Industrial Workers of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Workers_of_the_World "Industrial Workers of the World") left their organisations and joined the [Communist International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_International "Communist International").[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTENomad196688-57)

In the [Spanish Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War "Spanish Civil War") of 1936–39, anarchists and syndicalists ([CNT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confederaci%C3%B3n_Nacional_del_Trabajo "Confederación Nacional del Trabajo") and [FAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federaci%C3%B3n_Anarquista_Ib%C3%A9rica "Federación Anarquista Ibérica")) once again allied themselves with various currents of leftists. A long tradition of [Spanish anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_anarchism "Spanish anarchism") led to anarchists playing a pivotal role in the war, and particularly in the [Spanish Revolution of 1936](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Revolution_of_1936 "Spanish Revolution of 1936"). In response to the army [rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebellion "Rebellion"), an [anarchist-inspired movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Spain "Anarchism in Spain") of peasants and workers, supported by armed militias, took control of [Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barcelona "Barcelona") and of large areas of rural Spain, where they [collectivised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivised "Collectivised") the land.[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBolloten19841107-58) The [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union "Soviet Union") provided some limited assistance at the beginning of the war, but the result was a bitter fight between communists and other leftists in a series of events known as the [May Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_Days "May Days"), as [Joseph Stalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Stalin "Joseph Stalin") asserted Soviet control of the [Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_faction_\(Spanish_Civil_War\) "Republican faction (Spanish Civil War)") government, ending in another defeat of anarchists at the hands of the communists.[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993xi,_466-59)

#### Post-WWII

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=5 "Edit section: Post-WWII")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Rojava_Sewing_Cooperative.jpg/220px-Rojava_Sewing_Cooperative.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rojava_Sewing_Cooperative.jpg)

[Rojava's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_Administration_of_North_and_East_Syria "Autonomous Administration of North and East Syria") support efforts for workers to form cooperatives is exemplified in this sewing cooperative.

By the end of [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II "World War II"), the anarchist movement had been severely weakened.[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993xi-60) The 1960s witnessed a revival of anarchism, likely caused by a perceived failure of [Marxism–Leninism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism "Marxism–Leninism") and tensions built by the [Cold War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_War "Cold War").[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993539-61) During this time, anarchism found a presence in other movements critical towards both capitalism and the state such as the [anti-nuclear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-nuclear_movement "Anti-nuclear movement"), [environmental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_movement "Environmental movement"), and [peace movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_movement "Peace movement"), the [counterculture of the 1960s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterculture_of_the_1960s "Counterculture of the 1960s"), and the [New Left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Left "New Left").[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993xi,_539-62) It also saw a transition from its previous revolutionary nature to provocative [anti-capitalist reformism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-capitalist_reform "Anti-capitalist reform").[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy20115-63) Anarchism became associated with [punk subculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punk_subculture "Punk subculture") as exemplified by bands such as [Crass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crass "Crass") and the [Sex Pistols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_Pistols "Sex Pistols").[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993493%E2%80%93494-64) The established [feminist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminist "Feminist") tendencies of [anarcha-feminism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcha-feminism "Anarcha-feminism") returned with vigour during the [second wave of feminism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_wave_of_feminism "Second wave of feminism").[\[62\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993556%E2%80%93557-65) [Black anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_anarchism "Black anarchism") began to take form at this time and influenced anarchism's move from a [Eurocentric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocentric "Eurocentric") demographic.[\[63\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2015680-66) This coincided with its failure to gain traction in Northern Europe and its unprecedented height in Latin America.[\[64\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarmon201170-67)

Around the turn of the 21st century, anarchism grew in popularity and influence within anti-capitalist, [anti-war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-war_movement "Anti-war movement") and [anti-globalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-globalisation "Anti-globalisation") movements.[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTERupert200666-68) Anarchists became known for their involvement in protests against the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization "World Trade Organization") (WTO), the [Group of Eight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_Eight "Group of Eight") and the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Economic_Forum "World Economic Forum"). During the [protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protest "Protest"), *ad hoc* [leaderless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leaderless_resistance "Leaderless resistance") anonymous cadres known as [black blocs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_bloc "Black bloc") engaged in rioting, [property destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_destruction "Property destruction") and [violent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Violence "Violence") confrontations with the police. Other organisational tactics pioneered at this time include [affinity groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affinity_group "Affinity group"), [security culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_culture "Security culture") and the use of decentralised technologies such as the Internet. A significant event of this period was the confrontations at the [1999 Seattle WTO conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_Seattle_WTO_conference "1999 Seattle WTO conference").[\[65\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTERupert200666-68) Anarchist ideas have been influential in the development of the [Zapatistas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zapatista_Army_of_National_Liberation "Zapatista Army of National Liberation") in [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Mexico "Anarchism in Mexico") and the Democratic Federation of Northern Syria, more commonly known as [Rojava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_Administration_of_North_and_East_Syria "Autonomous Administration of North and East Syria"), a *de facto* [autonomous region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permanent_autonomous_zone "Permanent autonomous zone") in northern [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Syria "Anarchism in Syria").[\[66\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTERamnath2019691-69)

While having revolutionary aspirations, many contemporary forms of anarchism are not confrontational. Instead, they are trying to build an alternative way of [social organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_organization "Social organization") (following the theories of [dual power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_power "Dual power")), based on [mutual interdependence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual_aid "Mutual aid") and voluntary cooperation. Scholar Carissa Honeywell takes the example of [Food Not Bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_Not_Bombs "Food Not Bombs") group of collectives, to highlight some features of how contemporary anarchist groups work: [direct action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_action "Direct action"), working together and in solidarity with those left behind. While doing so, Food Not Bombs provides [consciousness raising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consciousness_raising "Consciousness raising") about the rising rates of world [hunger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunger "Hunger") and suggest policies to tackle hunger, ranging from [de-funding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funding "Funding") the [arms industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_industry "Arms industry") to addressing [Monsanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsanto "Monsanto") [seed-saving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seed_saving "Seed saving") policies and [patents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patent "Patent"), helping [farmers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmer "Farmer"), and resisting the [commodification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commodification "Commodification") of food and housing.[\[67\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoneywell202134%E2%80%9344-70) Honeywell also emphasizes that contemporary anarchists are interested in the flourishing not only of [humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human "Human"), but [non-humans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal "Animal") and the [environment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmentalism "Environmentalism") as well.[\[68\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoneywell20211%E2%80%932-71) Honeywell argues that their analysis of capitalism and governments results in anarchists [rejecting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-politics "Anti-politics") [representative democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_democracy "Representative democracy") and the state as a whole.[\[69\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHoneywell20211%E2%80%933-72)

## Schools of thought

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=6 "Edit section: Schools of thought")]

Anarchist schools of thought have been generally grouped into two main historical traditions, [social anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_anarchism "Social anarchism") and [individualist anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individualist_anarchism "Individualist anarchism"), owing to their different origins, [values](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Value_\(ethics_and_social_sciences\) "Value (ethics and social sciences)") and evolution.[\[70\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLeanMcMillan2003%22Anarchism%22Ostergaard200314%22Anarchism%22-73) The individualist current emphasises [negative liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Negative_liberty "Negative liberty") in opposing restraints upon the free individual, while the social current emphasises [positive liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Positive_liberty "Positive liberty") in aiming to achieve the free potential of society through equality and [social ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_ownership "Social ownership").[\[71\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarrisonBoyd2003251-74) In a chronological sense, anarchism can be segmented by the classical currents of the late 19th century and the post-classical currents ([anarcha-feminism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcha-feminism "Anarcha-feminism"), [green anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_anarchism "Green anarchism"), and [post-anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-anarchism "Post-anarchism")) developed thereafter.[\[72\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevyAdams20189-75)

Beyond the specific factions of anarchist movements which constitute political anarchism lies philosophical anarchism which holds that the state lacks [moral legitimacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legitimacy_\(political\) "Legitimacy (political)"), without necessarily accepting the imperative of revolution to eliminate it.[\[73\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEgoumenides20142-76) A component especially of individualist anarchism,[\[74\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEOstergaard200312Gabardi1986300%E2%80%93302-77) philosophical anarchism may tolerate the existence of a [minimal state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimal_state "Minimal state") but claims that citizens have no [moral obligation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moral_obligation "Moral obligation") to obey government when it conflicts with individual [autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomy "Autonomy").[\[75\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlosko20054-78) Anarchism pays significant attention to moral arguments since ethics have a central role in anarchist philosophy.[\[76\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranks2019549-79) Anarchism's emphasis on [anti-capitalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-capitalism "Anti-capitalism"), [egalitarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egalitarianism "Egalitarianism"), and for the extension of community and [individuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual "Individual") sets it apart from anarcho-capitalism and other types of [economic libertarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_libertarian "Economic libertarian").[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1992564%E2%80%93565Jennings1993143GayGay199915Morris200813Johnson2008169Franks2013393%E2%80%93394-22)

Anarchism is usually placed on the far-left of the political spectrum.[\[77\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrooks1994\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidgHyUj9HFYBICpgPP15_xi]-80) Much of its [economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchist_economics "Anarchist economics") and [legal philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchist_law "Anarchist law") reflect [anti-authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-authoritarian "Anti-authoritarian"), [anti-statist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-statist "Anti-statist"), [libertarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian "Libertarian"), and [radical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_politics "Radical politics") interpretations of left-wing and [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist "Socialist") politics[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGu%C3%A9rin197012-14) such as [collectivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivist_anarchism "Collectivist anarchism"), [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchist_communism "Anarchist communism"), [individualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individualist_anarchism "Individualist anarchism"), [mutualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutualism_\(economic_theory\) "Mutualism (economic theory)"), and [syndicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-syndicalism "Anarcho-syndicalism"), among other [libertarian socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libertarian_socialist "Libertarian socialist") economic theories.[\[78\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGu%C3%A9rin197035%22Critique_of_authoritarian_socialism%22-81) As anarchism does not offer a fixed body of [doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctrine "Doctrine") from a single particular [worldview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worldview "Worldview"),[\[79\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall199314%E2%80%9317-82) many anarchist types and traditions exist and varieties of anarchy diverge widely.[\[80\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTESylvan2007262-83) One reaction against [sectarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sectarianism "Sectarianism") within the anarchist milieu was [anarchism without adjectives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_without_adjectives "Anarchism without adjectives"), a call for [toleration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toleration "Toleration") and unity among anarchists first adopted by [Fernando Tarrida del Mármol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernando_Tarrida_del_M%C3%A1rmol "Fernando Tarrida del Mármol") in 1889 in response to the bitter debates of anarchist theory at the time.[\[81\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvrich19966-84) Belief in political [nihilism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nihilism "Nihilism") has been espoused by anarchists.[\[82\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWalter200252-85) Despite separation, the various anarchist schools of thought are not seen as distinct entities but rather as tendencies that intermingle and are connected through a set of shared principles such as autonomy, [mutual aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutual_aid "Mutual aid"), [anti-authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-authoritarianism "Anti-authoritarianism") and [decentralisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decentralization "Decentralization").[\[83\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall19931%E2%80%936AngelbeckGrier2012551-86)

### Classical

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=7 "Edit section: Classical")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Portrait_of_Pierre_Joseph_Proudhon_1865.jpg/170px-Portrait_of_Pierre_Joseph_Proudhon_1865.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Portrait_of_Pierre_Joseph_Proudhon_1865.jpg)

[Pierre-Joseph Proudhon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre-Joseph_Proudhon "Pierre-Joseph Proudhon") is the primary proponent of [mutualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutualism_\(economic_theory\) "Mutualism (economic theory)") and influenced many future [individualist anarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individualist_anarchism "Individualist anarchism") and [social anarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_anarchism "Social anarchism") thinkers.[\[84\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilbur2019216%E2%80%93218-87)

Inceptive currents among classical anarchist currents were [mutualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutualism_\(economic_theory\) "Mutualism (economic theory)") and [individualism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individualist_anarchism "Individualist anarchism"). They were followed by the major currents of social anarchism ([collectivist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectivist_anarchism "Collectivist anarchism"), [communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchist_communism "Anarchist communism") and [syndicalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-syndicalism "Anarcho-syndicalism")). They differ on [organisational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization "Organization") and economic aspects of their ideal society.[\[85\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevyAdams20182-88)

Mutualism is an 18th-century economic theory that was developed into anarchist theory by Pierre-Joseph Proudhon. Its aims include "abolishing the state",[\[86\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0-89) [reciprocity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reciprocity_\(cultural_anthropology\) "Reciprocity (cultural anthropology)"), [free association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_association_\(Marxism_and_anarchism\) "Free association (Marxism and anarchism)"), voluntary contract, federation and [monetary reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monetary_reform "Monetary reform") of both credit and currency that would be regulated by a bank of the people.[\[87\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilbur2019213%E2%80%93218-90) Mutualism has been retrospectively characterised as ideologically situated between individualist and collectivist forms of anarchism.[\[88\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvrich19966Miller199111-91) In *[What Is Property?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/What_Is_Property%3F "What Is Property?")* (1840), Proudhon first characterised his goal as a "third form of society, the synthesis of communism and property."[\[89\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPierson2013187-92) Collectivist anarchism is a [revolutionary socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_socialist "Revolutionary socialist") form of anarchism[\[90\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMorris199376-93) commonly associated with [Mikhail Bakunin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Bakunin "Mikhail Bakunin").[\[91\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShannon2019101-94) Collectivist anarchists advocate [collective ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_ownership "Collective ownership") of the [means of production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Means_of_production "Means of production") which is theorised to be achieved through violent revolution[\[92\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvrich19963%E2%80%934-95) and that workers be paid according to time worked, rather than goods being distributed according to need as in communism. Collectivist anarchism arose alongside [Marxism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism "Marxism") but rejected the [dictatorship of the proletariat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictatorship_of_the_proletariat "Dictatorship of the proletariat") despite the stated Marxist goal of a collectivist [stateless society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stateless_society "Stateless society").[\[93\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHeywood2017146%E2%80%93147Bakunin1990-96)

Anarcho-communism is a theory of anarchism that advocates a [communist society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_society "Communist society") with [common ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_ownership "Common ownership") of the means of production,[\[94\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMayne1999131-97) held by a [federal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism "Federalism") network of [voluntary associations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voluntary_association "Voluntary association"),[\[95\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993327-98) with production and consumption based on the guiding principle "[From each according to his ability, to each according to his need](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_each_according_to_his_ability,_to_each_according_to_his_need "From each according to his ability, to each according to his need")."[\[96\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993327Turcato2019237%E2%80%93238-99) Anarcho-communism developed from radical socialist currents after the [French Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution "French Revolution")[\[97\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham2005-100) but was first formulated as such in the [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Italy "Anarchism in Italy") section of the [First International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_International "First International").[\[98\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPernicone2009111%E2%80%93113-101) It was later expanded upon in the theoretical work of [Peter Kropotkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Kropotkin "Peter Kropotkin"),[\[99\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTETurcato2019239%E2%80%93244-102) whose specific style would go onto become the dominating view of anarchists by the late 19th century.[\[100\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy20116-103) Anarcho-syndicalism is a branch of anarchism that views [labour syndicates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_syndicate "Labour syndicate") as a potential force for revolutionary social change, replacing capitalism and the state with a new society democratically self-managed by workers. The basic principles of anarcho-syndicalism are [direct action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_action "Direct action"), workers' [solidarity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity "Solidarity") and [workers' self-management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workers%27_self-management "Workers' self-management").[\[101\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_der_Walt2019249-104)

Individualist anarchism is a set of several traditions of thought within the anarchist movement that emphasise the [individual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual "Individual") and their [will](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_\(philosophy\) "Will (philosophy)") over any kinds of external determinants.[\[102\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTERyley2019225-105) Early influences on individualist forms of anarchism include [William Godwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Godwin "William Godwin"), [Max Stirner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Stirner "Max Stirner"), and [Henry David Thoreau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_David_Thoreau "Henry David Thoreau"). Through many countries, individualist anarchism attracted a small yet diverse following of Bohemian artists and intellectuals[\[103\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarshall1993440-106) as well as young anarchist outlaws in what became known as [illegalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegalism "Illegalism") and [individual reclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual_reclamation "Individual reclamation").[\[104\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEImrie1994Parry198715-107)

### Post-classical and contemporary

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=8 "Edit section: Post-classical and contemporary")]

Main article: [Contemporary anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_anarchism "Contemporary anarchism")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ed/Jarach_and_Zerzan.JPG/220px-Jarach_and_Zerzan.JPG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jarach_and_Zerzan.JPG)

Lawrence Jarach (left) and [John Zerzan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Zerzan "John Zerzan") (right) are two prominent contemporary anarchist authors, with Zerzan being a prominent voice within [anarcho-primitivism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-primitivism "Anarcho-primitivism") and Jarach a notable advocate of [post-left anarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Post-left_anarchy "Post-left anarchy").

Anarchist principles undergird contemporary radical [social movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_movement "Social movement") of the left. Interest in the anarchist movement developed alongside momentum in the anti-globalisation movement,[\[105\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEvren20111-108) whose leading activist networks were anarchist in orientation.[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEvren20112-109) As the movement shaped 21st century radicalism, wider embrace of anarchist principles signaled a revival of interest.[\[106\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEvren20112-109) Anarchism has continued to generate many philosophies and movements, at times eclectic, drawing upon various sources and [combining disparate concepts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syncretic "Syncretic") to create new philosophical approaches.[\[107\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2007303-110) The anti-capitalist tradition of classical anarchism has remained prominent within contemporary currents.[\[108\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20184-111)

Contemporary news coverage which emphasizes [black bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_bloc "Black bloc") demonstrations has reinforced anarchism's historical association with chaos and violence. Its publicity has also led more scholars in fields such as [anthropology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropology "Anthropology") and [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historian "Historian") to engage with the anarchist movement, although contemporary anarchism favours actions over [academic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy "Academy") theory.[\[109\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2010110Evren20111AngelbeckGrier2012549-112) Various anarchist groups, tendencies, and schools of thought exist today, making it difficult to describe the contemporary anarchist movement.[\[110\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranks2013385%E2%80%93386-113) While theorists and activists have established "relatively stable constellations of anarchist principles", there is no consensus on which principles are core and commentators describe multiple *anarchisms*, rather than a singular *anarchism*, in which common principles are shared between schools of anarchism while each group prioritizes those principles differently. [Gender equality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_equality "Gender equality") can be a common principle, although it ranks as a higher priority to anarcha-feminists than anarcho-communists.[\[111\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranks2013386-114)

Anarchists are generally committed against coercive authority in all forms, namely "all centralized and [hierarchical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hierarchical_organization "Hierarchical organization") forms of government (e.g., monarchy, representative democracy, state socialism, etc.), economic class systems (e.g., capitalism, [Bolshevism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshevism "Bolshevism"), [feudalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feudalism "Feudalism"), [slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery "Slavery"), etc.), autocratic religions (e.g., [fundamentalist Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_fundamentalism "Islamic fundamentalism"), [Roman Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church "Catholic Church"), etc.), [patriarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriarchy "Patriarchy"), [heterosexism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heterosexism "Heterosexism"), [white supremacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_supremacy "White supremacy"), and [imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperialism "Imperialism")."[\[112\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJun2009507%E2%80%93508-115) Anarchist schools disagree on the methods by which these forms should be opposed.[\[113\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJun2009507-116) The principle of [equal liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equal_liberty "Equal liberty") is closer to anarchist political ethics in that it transcends both the liberal and socialist traditions. This entails that liberty and equality cannot be implemented within the state, resulting in the questioning of all forms of domination and hierarchy.[\[114\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEgoumenides201491-117)

## Tactics

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=9 "Edit section: Tactics")]

Anarchists' tactics take various forms but in general serve two major goals, namely, to first oppose [the Establishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Establishment "The Establishment") and secondly to promote anarchist ethics and reflect an anarchist vision of society, illustrating the [unity of means and ends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instrumental_and_intrinsic_value "Instrumental and intrinsic value").[\[115\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019107%E2%80%93108-118) A broad categorisation can be made between aims to destroy oppressive states and institutions by revolutionary means on one hand and aims to change society through evolutionary means on the other.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20184%E2%80%935-119) Evolutionary tactics embrace [nonviolence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonviolence "Nonviolence") and take a gradual approach to anarchist aims, although there is significant overlap between the two.[\[117\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinna2019125-120)

Anarchist tactics have shifted during the course of the last century. Anarchists during the early 20th century focused more on strikes and militancy while contemporary anarchists use a [broader array of approaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diversity_of_tactics "Diversity of tactics").[\[118\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019112-121)

### Classical era

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=10 "Edit section: Classical era")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/McKinleyAssassination.jpg/220px-McKinleyAssassination.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:McKinleyAssassination.jpg)

The relationship between [anarchism and violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_and_violence "Anarchism and violence") is a controversial subject among anarchists as shown by anarchist [Leon Czolgosz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon_Czolgosz "Leon Czolgosz") [assassinating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_William_McKinley "Assassination of William McKinley") [William McKinley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_McKinley "William McKinley").

During the classical era, anarchists had a militant tendency. Not only did they confront state armed forces, as in Spain and Ukraine, but some of them also employed terrorism as [propaganda of the deed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propaganda_of_the_deed "Propaganda of the deed"). Assassination attempts were carried out against [heads of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state "Head of state"), some of which were successful. Anarchists also took part in revolutions.[\[119\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019112%E2%80%93113-122) Many anarchists, especially the [Galleanists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galleanists "Galleanists"), believed that these attempts would be the impetus for a revolution against capitalism and the state.[\[120\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTENorris20207%E2%80%938-123) Many of these attacks were done by individual assailants and the majority took place in the late 1870s, the early 1880s and the 1890s, with some still occurring in the early 1900s.[\[121\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy201113Nesser201262-124) Their decrease in prevalence was the result of further judicial power and of targeting and cataloging by state institutions.[\[122\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarmon201155-125)

Anarchist perspectives towards violence have always been controversial.[\[123\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarter1978320-126) [Anarcho-pacifists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarcho-pacifism "Anarcho-pacifism") advocate for non-violence means to achieve their stateless, nonviolent ends.[\[124\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFiala2017section_3.1-127) Other anarchist groups advocate [direct action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_action "Direct action"), a tactic which can include acts of [sabotage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabotage "Sabotage") or [terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism "Terrorism"). This attitude was quite prominent a century ago when seeing the state as a [tyrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyrant "Tyrant") and some anarchists believing that they had every right to oppose its oppression by any means possible.[\[125\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinna2019116%E2%80%93117-128) [Emma Goldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emma_Goldman "Emma Goldman") and [Errico Malatesta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Errico_Malatesta "Errico Malatesta"), who were proponents of limited use of violence, stated that violence is merely a reaction to state violence as a [necessary evil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Necessary_evil "Necessary evil").[\[126\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarter1978320%E2%80%93325-129)

Anarchists took an active role in strike actions, although they tended to be antipathetic to formal [syndicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syndicalism "Syndicalism"), seeing it as [reformist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reformist "Reformist"). They saw it as a part of the movement which sought to overthrow the state and capitalism.[\[127\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019113-130) Anarchists also reinforced their propaganda [within the arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_and_the_arts "Anarchism and the arts"), some of whom practiced [naturism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturism "Naturism") and [nudism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nudism "Nudism"). Those anarchists also built communities which were based on friendship and were involved in the news media.[\[128\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019114-131)

### Revolutionary

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=11 "Edit section: Revolutionary")]

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Black_bloc_at_RNC_running.jpg/220px-Black_bloc_at_RNC_running.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Black_bloc_at_RNC_running.jpg)

Black bloc protesters parading anarcho-communism imagery such as the motto "[No War but the Class War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_War_but_the_Class_War "No War but the Class War")"

In the current era, [Italian anarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Italy "Anarchism in Italy") [Alfredo Bonanno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfredo_Bonanno "Alfredo Bonanno"), a proponent of [insurrectionary anarchism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurrectionary_anarchism "Insurrectionary anarchism"), has reinstated the debate on violence by rejecting the nonviolence tactic adopted since the late 19th century by Kropotkin and other prominent anarchists afterwards. Both Bonanno and the French group [The Invisible Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Invisible_Committee "The Invisible Committee") advocate for small, informal affiliation groups, where each member is responsible for their own actions but works together to bring down oppression using [sabotage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabotage "Sabotage") and other violent means against state, capitalism, and other enemies. [Members of The Invisible Committee were arrested in 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarnac_Nine "Tarnac Nine") on various charges, terrorism included.[\[129\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinna2019134%E2%80%93135-132)

Overall, contemporary anarchists are much less violent and militant than their ideological ancestors. They mostly engage in confronting the police during demonstrations and riots, especially in countries such as [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Canada "Anarchism in Canada"), [Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Greece "Anarchism in Greece"), and [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Mexico "Anarchism in Mexico"). Militant black bloc protest groups are known for clashing with the police;[\[130\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019115-133) however, anarchists not only clash with state operators, they also engage in the struggle against [fascists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism "Fascism") and [racists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism "Racism"), taking [anti-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-fascist "Anti-fascist") action and mobilizing to prevent hate rallies from happening.[\[131\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019117-134)

### Evolutionary

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Anarchism\&action=edit\&section=12 "Edit section: Evolutionary")]

Anarchists commonly employ [direct action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_action "Direct action"). This can take the form of disrupting and protesting against unjust hierarchy, or the form of self-managing their lives through the creation of counter-institutions such as [communes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intentional_community "Intentional community") and non-hierarchical collectives.[\[116\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams20184%E2%80%935-119) Decision-making is often handled in an anti-authoritarian way, with everyone having [equal say in each decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_decision-making "Group decision-making"), an approach known as [horizontalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horizontalidad "Horizontalidad").[\[132\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019109%E2%80%93117-135) Contemporary-era anarchists have been engaging with various [grassroots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grassroots "Grassroots") movements that are more or less based on horizontalism, although not explicitly anarchist, respecting personal autonomy and participating in mass activism such as strikes and demonstrations. In contrast with the "big-A Anarchism" of the classical era, the newly coined term "small-a anarchism" signals their tendency not to base their thoughts and actions on classical-era anarchism or to refer to classical anarchists such as Peter Kropotkin and Pierre-Joseph Proudhon to justify their opinions. Those anarchists would rather base their thought and praxis on their own experience, which they will later theorize.[\[133\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKinna2019145%E2%80%93149-136)

The concept of [prefigurative politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prefigurative_politics "Prefigurative politics") is enacted by many contemporary anarchist groups, striving to embody the principles, organization and tactics of the changed social structure they hope to bring about. As part of this the decision-making process of small anarchist affinity groups plays a significant tactical role.[\[134\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019109,_119-137) Anarchists have employed various methods to build a rough consensus among members of their group without the need of a leader or a leading group. One way is for an individual from the group to play the role of [facilitator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Facilitator "Facilitator") to help achieve a [consensus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consensus_decision-making "Consensus decision-making") without taking part in the discussion themselves or promoting a specific point. Minorities usually accept rough consensus, except when they feel the proposal contradicts anarchist ethics, goals and values. Anarchists usually form small groups (5–20 individuals) to enhance autonomy and friendships among their members. These kinds of groups more often than not interconnect with each other, forming larger networks. Anarchists still support and participate in strikes, especially [wildcat strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildcat_strike_action "Wildcat strike action") as these are leaderless strikes not organised centrally by a syndicate.[\[135\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilliams2019119%E2%80%93121-138)
